# Pipeline Backlog

> **Findings & intake register for the documentation-driven-development pipeline.** Two writers:
> **`00-pipeline-manager`** (harvests every invoked skill's findings/recommendations/Outstanding
> Issues after each run, triages every open entry at the start of the next run, and flips
> statuses) and **`00-intake`** (appends `NEW` entries for user-submitted features, bugs, and
> observations). Stage skills never write here — their findings reach this file via the manager's
> harvest step, which is what guarantees a finding stated in a chat summary survives the session
> that stated it. Rows are never deleted: resolved entries flip to `DONE`, rejected ones to
> `REJECTED` with the reason kept.

[↑ Docs index](../INDEX.md) · [Pipeline journal](pipeline-journal.md) ·
[Pipeline README](../../.claude/skills/README.md)

## Lifecycle

```
NEW ──triage──▶ SCHEDULED (rides a named step)  ──that step runs──▶ DONE
        ├─────▶ DEFERRED  (named revisit trigger) ──trigger fires──▶ NEW (re-triage)
        ├─────▶ NEEDS-USER (named decision)       ──user decides───▶ SCHEDULED / REJECTED
        └─────▶ REJECTED  (reason recorded)
IN PIPELINE = the entry's work is the current/next step's explicit target
```

Every open entry carries a live disposition — `SCHEDULED` names the step it rides with,
`DEFERRED` names its revisit trigger, `NEEDS-USER` names the exact decision required. A
Critical/High entry may not be `DEFERRED` without the user's explicit agreement.

| BL-0052 | 2026-07-05 | feature | intake: project owner, extensive scoping discussion this session (research pass + 4 AskUserQuestion design-fork decisions) | **Vignette Creator — a large, distinct White-Cell authoring feature, anchored to (but explicitly not blended into) `FEAT-5100` (In-App Iterative Vignette Builder, `EP-5000`, 0% built — no FS, no code).** Project owner's own words: "I want a JSON view, a 2D/3D views of the initial state. Review research for all JSON vignette parameters to ensure there is an optimal entry method for the white cell. There should be a TLE entry box, lat/long entry box for ground infrastructure, when entering tle and lat/long a selector for asset type and cell assignment and name entry. ROE selectors per cell. There should be an asset menu where assets can be edited, reassigned, deleted. Payload and bus parameter defaults can be overwritten (eg effective range of payload, fuel level). Identify any missing research that needs to be completed to ensure all bus, payload, and ground infrastructure parameters are captured, listed in the JSON, determines the variable type/range, and available in the vignette creator providing an effective way of entering/editing the information." Explicit phasing: **v1 (this entry's scope) is authoring-time only — no exercise running.** A later, explicitly deferred phase reuses the same/similar menu surface alongside the existing White-Cell inject mechanism for mid-exercise parameter edits and asset add/remove — linked, not in scope now. **Grounding research performed this session** (Explore-agent read of the live tree): EP-5000 catalogs FEAT-5100/5200 (TLE-add endpoint exists, sgp4-validated, pre-start-only, zero UI wiring, no real Space-Track integration despite release-plan's "RTM-verified built" claim)/5300 (spec'd, unimplemented), none built; `CR-11` (already an open Candidate Requirement) asks exactly where in-progress vignette-authoring state should live, unresolved until now; ground stations are plain `Asset` entries with `location:{lat_deg,lon_deg,alt_m}`, no dedicated schema, but `docs/vignettes/GROUND-INFRASTRUCTURE.md` curates ~45 real sites for reuse; all 8 payload types share one identical generic `PayloadState` model, no type-specific fields; ROE is two global `Parameter`s (`red_kinetic_authorized`, `cyber_authorized`) enforced globally in `engine/orders.py` regardless of issuing cell, not actually per-cell; confirmed research gap — no doc enumerates realistic bus/payload parameter ranges, no TLE-format primer, no ground-siting methodology beyond the coordinate list. **Four design-fork decisions the project owner made directly (AskUserQuestion), binding on whoever picks this up:** (1) draft-authoring state is a **server-side draft session** — an unstarted `SessionManager` instance in memory, reusing existing engine/session machinery (TLE-add endpoint, etc.), serialized to YAML on Save; the 2D/3D initial-state preview should reuse the existing render-from-custody scene/globe/map pipeline in ground-truth mode, not a bespoke visualization — this resolves `CR-11`; (2) payload parameters get **typed per-payload-type sub-schemas** (e.g. an ISR sub-schema's `effective_range_km` distinct from SATCOM's bandwidth field), a real Domain Model change, not the generic dict kept as-is — this is exactly where the missing parameter-range research needs to land; (3) **build real per-cell ROE enforcement now** (not a cosmetic per-cell selector over today's global flags) — changes the Domain Model and `engine/orders.py`'s currently-global check, touching already-`VERIFIED` `FS-101`/`FS-102` territory; (4) **documentation route: a new `ADS-xxx` design-synthesis cluster first (Workflow B, like `ADS-3500`), then a consolidated Feature Specification** that likely supersedes `FEAT-5100`'s FS and clarifies its boundary against `FEAT-5200`/`FEAT-5300`, rather than blending into any of the three. **Decided (2026-07-05, follow-up):** Space-Track.org network integration is explicitly **deferred** — out of this feature's v1 scope entirely, left as `FEAT-5200`'s own separate, not-yet-scheduled concern. **TLE entry's primary/only mode for v1 is manual paste**, matching the backend TLE-add endpoint's existing sgp4-validated behavior — no auto-import UI to build now. **Still-undecided items to land in that ADS-xxx/FS pass:** a raw JSON view/editor; lat/long entry for ground infrastructure (should probably offer picking from the existing curated site list, not just free entry); asset-type/cell-assignment/name-entry selectors alongside TLE/lat-long entry; an asset menu (edit/reassign/delete); bus parameter defaults (e.g. fuel level) overridable, same treatment as payload sub-schemas. | BL-0123 | 2026-09-27 | finding | [VR-1174](../implementation/verification/VR-1174-vignette-creator-ui-surfaces.md) (fresh-agent verification, Finding H1) | **`FR-5160`'s per-cell seat declaration is not delivered — only White-Cell seats can ever be created.** `POST /creator/seats` overloads the request body's `cell` as both the caller's required identity (`!= "white"` → 403) and the target cell the seats are declared for, so choosing Blue/Red in the Creator UI throws an uncaught 403; the matrix's `roles/assign` call has the same defect, sending the seat's own cell prefix as caller identity. `test_seat_declaration_rejects_non_white_cell` pins the defect as intended behavior. | BL-0134 | 2026-09-27 | finding | [VR-1210](../implementation/verification/VR-1210-ephemeris-export.md) (fresh-agent verification, Finding H1) | **The exported RIC velocity is wrong — it is the inertial relative velocity resolved onto RIC axes, missing the frame-rotation term.** `to_ric()` returns `basis @ (v_target − v_ref)`; the RIC frame itself rotates with the reference orbit, so the true RIC-frame relative velocity is `basis @ (v_t − v_r) − ω × ρ_ric`. Probe: two satellites on the same orbit 0.1° apart (constant RIC position) report −13.29 m/s radial velocity — exactly the missing `ω × ρ` term. Affects both `FR-7410` (truth) and `FR-7420` (cell-observed) CSV output; no existing test checks velocity. | **High** | 07/08 | `IP-1210` flips `VERIFIED → IN PROGRESS` (returned). Fix direction from the VR: subtract `ω × ρ` (ω from the reference's own `h/|r|²`, or its exact rate if eccentric); add a co-orbital-fixture test asserting `ric_v ≈ 0`, plus a finite-difference-of-`ric_r` cross-check. Alternatively, if "inertial relative velocity in RIC axes" is the deliberately intended convention, the column must be renamed/documented and the package/FS changed — an owner decision either way, not something to leave implicit. **Authorized 2026-09-27 by the project owner (MSTR-006 §3, batched with IP-1174/IP-1062 remediation authorization) — fix to true RIC-frame velocity (not rename/document as inertial).** Remediated 2026-09-27/28: `to_ric()` now subtracts `ω × ric_r` (`ω = |h_ref| / |r_ref|²` along `n_hat`, expressed in the RIC basis as `[0, 0, ω]`). Added `test_to_ric_velocity_zero_for_co_orbital_ric_stationary_neighbour` (the exact reported probe case — now reports `ric_v ≈ 0`) and `test_to_ric_velocity_matches_finite_difference_of_ric_position` (general-case cross-check; a small sub-m/s residual remains from the reference orbit's own J2 nodal precession, a separate, much smaller effect than the ω×ρ term this fix targets — documented in the test). Full suite green (both permanent gates included). **Independently re-verified 2026-09-28 (`VR-1210` v2.0, run #81): fix confirmed by an independent from-scratch numeric re-derivation** (not merely a test re-run) for an eccentric/inclined geometry distinct from the package's own tests — matched to 1e-6. `IP-1210` flips `IN PROGRESS → VERIFIED`. | DONE |

| BL-0170 | 2026-10-10 intake: live telemetry series route back-dates current world state across its whole time window; investigate holistically and plan a fix (no code) | bug | Project-owner request (this session), following up `BL-0168` part (2) / [VR-1260](../implementation/verification/VR-1260-telemetry-csv-export.md), which named the quirk as pre-existing but filed no entry for it (and no other tracker holds it: `docs/FUTURE-WORK.md`, `docs/AUDIT-2026-06.md`, and this backlog were grepped) | **Defect.** `SessionManager.get_series` (`spacesim/session/manager.py:857-866`, behind `GET /api/sessions/{sid}/telemetry/{cell}/{asset}/{param}`, `ui_web/server.py:779`) passes the live `self.sim.world` to `engine/telemetry.py::series()`, which calls `sample(world, ..., t)` for every `t` in `[t0, t1]` (default: the last hour). `sample()` is only time-aware for effect flags (`_active()` filters `world.active_effects` by `start <= t <= end`; that list is append-only, never pruned) and for the seeded noise; everything else reads *current* state: `health == "destroyed"` (a kill turns the whole window LOS), `_baseline()` (`battery_soc`, `storage_frac`, `propellant_frac`, `bus_voltage_v` via `power.status`/`in_eclipse`, `array_current_a`), `_mitigation()` (`interference_mitigation`, `freq_hopping` — anti-jam ordered after a jam retroactively shrinks the past jam signature), and `_attack_term`'s `safed` gate (`snr_db`, `attitude_error_deg`, cyber-cause `cpu_load_pct`, and the `fsw_error_count`/`cmd_reject_count` fallback start). The `nominal=1` ghost is affected too via `_baseline()`. **Reproduced (intake, read-only scratch script driving `SessionManager` and the HTTP route on `training-basics`, seed 1, clock paused; each `get_series` point compared with `telemetry.sample()` on `aar.state_at_time(mgr, t)`):** (A) bus `anomaly` inject at +600 s, run to +1200 s, n=7: `snr_db` pre-onset points read about 1.4-2.3 dB `red` vs replay-correct about 29.4-30.4 dB `green` (3/7 differ); `attitude_error_deg` reads about 2.53 `yellow` vs about 0.03-0.05 `green` (3/7); `battery_soc` reads 0.995/0.9999 vs 0.895/0.8999 (2/7, the current SoC back-dated). (B) same with `cause: cyber`: `cpu_load_pct` pre-onset reads about 74-76 vs about 29-31 (3/7). `fsw_error_count` matched 0/7 differ at onset (its `t >= start` gate is time-aware), **correcting `VR-1260`'s list**: the fallback does not leak backward at onset, but by code inspection it erases itself from history after recovery (`exit_safe_mode` resets `safe_mode`, so `start` becomes `None` for every `t`). (C) anomaly at +300 s, cleared at +600 s, run to +900 s: the safe-mode episode **vanishes from history**, so `snr_db` at +300/+450 s reads about 30.3 `green` vs replay-correct about 2.3 `red` (the inverse failure: symptoms disappear rather than leak). (D) the real HTTP route as the console calls it (`?n=120`, default window) after a 600 s step, inject, 600 s step: all 60 of 60 pre-onset points `red` (1.43-2.80 dB), while `export.csv` over the same session shows `snr_db` 29.4/30.3/30.1 `green` before onset and `red` after. The two shipped routes disagree for every historical point, against `FR-2320`'s Postcondition ("identical to what the same cell's own live telemetry endpoint would show at each sampled point"). **Call-site scope** (every caller of `telemetry.sample`/`series`/`telemetry_db`/`subsystem_log`, plus every other time-ranged session read). *Quirky (one site):* `get_series` (above). *Correct:* `get_telemetry` and `alarms` (sample at `clock.now` on the live world, so `t` matches the state); `telemetry_export` (`manager.py:868-908`; replays per distinct event-log position); `ephemeris.truth_ephemeris`/`cell_observed_ephemeris` (`aar.state_at_time` per sample, correct but **unmemoized**: one full replay per sample); `aar.state_at`/`state_at_time`/`objectives_at`/`snapshot_at`/`AARPlayback.state` (replay-based, correct); `scene.build_scene` (state at `now` plus a *forward* predicted ground track from the current orbit, which is prediction, not history); `get_ric_view`/`get_tle` (point-in-time now); `windows_ahead`/`next_contacts` (forward); `maneuver_ledger` (event-log-derived); `session/assessment.py` (reads decision-time values recorded at issue, by design); `tests/test_telemetry.py`, `test_bus_commands.py`, `test_future_work.py` (unit calls on hand-built worlds at their own `now`). **Why it is this way:** no rationale is recorded anywhere. `telemetry.py` and `get_series` arrived wholesale in `2c9ed07` (a training-doc commit importing the tree) and `3444ad5` (re-adds the file); `85f1762` is R121 research only. The module docstring's "pure read-time derived signal (like `build_scene`)" fits a now-only reading; the windowed series looks like an unexamined extension of it, not a deliberate tradeoff. **Performance facts (measured):** `graph.js` itself does not poll. `app.js` polls `refresh()` every 1.5 s but rebuilds the drill-down (`openDrill`, `app.js:1316`) only when the asset's `last_tel` changes (a new telemetry contact). Each rebuild issues about 21 series calls (19 sparklines at `n=120`, the main graph, plus optional ghost/overlay), and `drawParam` re-fires on param/overlay/nominal clicks. Each call runs under the per-session `RLock` (`inprocess.py::_locked_read`), so read cost is lock-hold time that blocks other tabs. **No production code ever calls `Simulation.snapshot()`** (only `test_determinism.py`), so `sim.snapshots` is always empty in a live session; `replay()`'s snapshot shortcut and `telemetry_export`'s `snapshots=` argument are no-ops in practice and every replay starts from scratch, so cost is O(event-log length). Measured on `multi-domain-taiwan` at +6 h (73 entries, mostly 300 s `bus_tick`): current-world `get_series` n=120 about 0.9 ms; one full replay about 3.6 ms; the default 1 h window spans 13 distinct event-log positions, so a naive memoized-per-seq series costs about 46 ms per call, or about 1 s of lock hold per drill rebuild, growing linearly with session length (on `training-basics`, a replay costs about 0.5 ms). **Options evaluated.** (1) Copy `telemetry_export`'s per-seq replay into `get_series`: correct, but about 13 from-scratch replays per call and about 21 calls per rebuild under the lock, worsening as the log grows. Rejected alone. (2) **Single forward pass:** replay once to the window's first event-log position, then apply only the events between consecutive sorted sample times to that one private world, sampling as it goes. O(log length) per call regardless of `n`, so 13 replays become about 1, with exactly the per-instant state `state_at_time` gives (`sample()` reads state plus `t`, not `world.now`). (3) **Live-tail shortcut:** samples at or after the last entry's `sim_time` (and any `t > now`, which today's route already serves as a current-state prediction) read the live world with zero replay, since state only changes through logged events. Correct and free, but the tail is short (at most one bus-tick period), so it cannot stand alone. (4) **Shared helper** (for example, a `worlds_at_times(mgr, times)` generator next to `aar.state_at_time`) combining (2)+(3), used by `get_series`, `telemetry_export`, and both ephemeris exports (and `AARPlayback` if useful). It removes three hand-rolled implementations, speeds up ephemeris (today one full replay per sample), and leaves one correct path for a fourth caller. Low risk: `test_telemetry.py` already asserts `telemetry_export` equals `sample()` on `state_at_time` at every point, `test_ephemeris.py` has 29 tests and `test_aar.py` has 9, and the helper's own contract (its world at `t` equals `state_at_time(t)`) is directly property-testable. (5) Production snapshotting (`sim.snapshot()` every K events) to bound every replay at O(K): it would help AAR/ephemeris/export/series alike, but it changes `SavedSession` size and memory and sits beside the determinism gate. **Defer** to a separate, measured perf item unless (4) shows lock-hold regressions on long sessions. (6) An LRU cache of replayed worlds keyed by (rewind generation, seq): unnecessary once (2) collapses each call to one pass, and it adds invalidation risk on rewind/undo. Rejected for now. (7) Time-index the state telemetry reads (persist bus-mode/SoC/mitigation/health histories so `sample(live_world, t)` is correct without replay): adds persisted state, has to cover every state-dependent field (whack-a-mole), and diverges from the export's replay semantics. Rejected. (8) Document it as a limitation or fix it UI-side: contradicts `FR-2320`'s cross-route parity and keeps misleading the core "diagnose from symptoms" training loop (MSTR-001, R121). Rejected. **Recommendation for `07`:** one remediation package implementing (4) = (2)+(3): route `get_series` through the shared single-pass helper, and migrate `telemetry_export` and the two ephemeris exports onto it with outputs byte-identical to today. Regression tests should cover scenarios A-D above (pre-onset `snr_db`/`attitude_error_deg`/`cpu_load_pct`/`battery_soc` match replay; a cleared episode stays visible in history; a destroyed asset is not LOS before its kill; route series equals `export.csv` at shared instants), plus a cost guard showing the replay count per series call is constant rather than proportional to distinct seqs. Determinism and read-only invariants are unchanged (all reads use a private replayed copy, never `sim.world`, with no RNG draw and no wall clock). **Anchor note for `07`:** no GDS-05/`FR` names the live series route itself (it descends from legacy `build-spec/07-operator-console.md`). The binding hooks are `FR-2320`'s Postcondition (parity) and `FR-2310` (timestamped telemetry). If `07` judges that anchor insufficient, route a candidate requirement to `04` first. **Adjacent observation, not part of this bug, not adjudicated:** neither `get_series` nor `telemetry_export` applies `FR-2310` pass-gating to historical points (values between contacts are shown), even though `FR-2320` describes the export as "pass-gated". Flagged for `07`/`09` to confirm whether that is the intended reading. | Medium (evidence: operator-visible wrong history in the drill-down graphs and sparklines; a cross-route inconsistency with `FR-2320`; no crash, no fog breach). No user-stated priority | 09 (`09-package-verification` on `IP-1261`, fresh session; was 07 → 08) | **Triaged 2026-10-11 (`00-pipeline-manager`, run #98, `triage` mode).** The entry's own "Anchor note for `07`" is resolved, not deferred: the project owner has now explicitly confirmed (this session's task) that the windowed console telemetry graphs (the UI feature `get_series` serves) are a desired, kept feature — there is no "should this capability exist" question left to route to `04` first. `FR-2320`'s Postcondition (cross-route parity) + `FR-2310` (timestamped telemetry) are accepted as sufficient requirements anchor for `07` to plan the remediation package against directly; no `04` pre-step is needed for the core back-dating fix. | BL-0173 | 2026-10-11 | finding | `00-pipeline-manager` (run #100, harvested from `08-code-implementation` on `IP-1261`, Outstanding Issues) | `IP-1261`'s `get_series` handles a caller-supplied reversed window (`t1 < t0`) by sampling its instants in ascending order and then restoring the original descending point order. The package text does not mention this case; without it the helper's non-decreasing-times rule would turn such a request (which the pre-`IP-1261` route answered with descending points) into a `500`. The handling keeps the old route behaviour, so it was judged in scope, but it is a small addition the package did not list. | Low | 09 (`09-package-verification` on `IP-1261`) | **Adjudicated 2026-10-11 (`09-package-verification`, `VR-1261`, fresh session, independent re-derivation — not a re-read of the implementer's own claim).** Independently confirmed real and correctly handled: built a fresh scenario (seed `606`) and called `get_series` both forward and reversed (`t0`/`t1` swapped); the reversed call did not raise, its timestamps were the forward call's in reverse order, and every shared instant's value/status matched exactly. `engine/simulation.py::replay_stepwise`'s and `session/aar.py::worlds_at_times`'s non-decreasing-input contract is unaffected — `get_series` pre-sorts to ascending before calling the helper and reverses the output after, entirely within `manager.py`. **Accepted as necessary-to-preserve-behaviour, no remediation to `IP-1261` itself.** One residual gap filed: no regression test anywhere in the suite exercises this path (`grep -rn "backwards\|reversed" spacesim/tests/test_telemetry.py spacesim/tests/test_web.py` returns nothing) — a future refactor could silently break it with nothing failing. Routed as a small, additive follow-up test, filed as `BL-0174`. | DONE |

A future refactor of `get_series`, `aar.worlds_at_times`, or `engine.replay_stepwise`'s non-decreasing-input contract could silently reintroduce a crash (a `ValueError` surfacing as a 500) or a wrong point order on a reversed window, with the full suite staying green throughout. Recommended fix: one small test in `test_telemetry.py` (or `test_web.py`, mirroring Test 10's route-level style) asserting a reversed-window `get_series` call doesn't raise and its points match a forward call over the same span, reversed. | Low | 08 (`08-code-implementation`, a small additive test-only change — no production code implicated) | **Run #104: folded into [`IP-1262`](../implementation/packages/IP-1262-export-sampling-bounds.md) as its rider Test 7** (entry stage `08`). | BL-0176 | 2026-10-11 | bug | `00-pipeline-manager` (run #103, harvested from `09-training-manual-review`, [REV-TRN-002](../reviews/training-review-run97-scope.md) F1) | Export `interval_s` has no lower bound: `session/ephemeris.py::sample_times` floors the step at 1 µs, `GET /ephemeris/truth` and `GET /ephemeris/{cell}` accept `0`/negative `interval_s`, and `GET /telemetry/{cell}/{asset}/export.csv`'s `gt=0` still accepts e.g. `1e-6`. `sample_times(0, 10 s, 0.0)` builds 10,000,001 instants; over a real span the request replays billions of instants under the session read lock, freezing the exercise for every client. Was: next `07-implementation-planning` pass; Medium, ungated for planning, implementation needs MSTR-006 §3 authorization (covered by the 2026-10-11 session pre-authorization if reached in that iteration). **Run #105 (2026-10-11): `IP-1262` `COMPLETE` (commit `4fd4591`)** — entry stage now `09` (fresh-session `09-package-verification` on `IP-1262`). **Run #106 (2026-10-11, fresh-session `09-package-verification`, `VR-1262`, `IP-1262` → `VERIFIED`): the DoS-shaped defect independently confirmed closed, not merely claimed closed. Read `sample_times`'s code directly (the arithmetic count check precedes list construction at every one of its three call sites, confirmed by direct `grep`/read, not by trusting the docstring) and timed rejection of requests implying ~10¹⁵–10²⁴ instants (six orders of magnitude past the implementer's own ~10⁷ timing test): 0.003–0.005 ms at the module level, 3.49 ms through a full HTTP round trip, with no growth tied to the attempted count — the signature of O(1) arithmetic, not a disguised generate-then-reject. All `interval_s` boundary values (`0`, negative, `nan`, ±`inf`) and the exact-ceiling/one-over boundary independently reproduced on fresh scenarios distinct from the implementer's fixtures. Fail-first claim independently reproduced on a detached pre-fix worktree (`daa1915` + new test files only: 8/10 new cases failed, matching exactly). Full suite 833 passed/3 skipped, both gates green, independently re-run.** | DONE |

Contradicts `FR-4420` Outputs ("the condition's evaluated value") and `IP-1064` Objective 1 / Design Decision 1. Fire decision, fire-once, effects and replay are unaffected (informational field).
